# Rule re-check against the 0.22 sources (#634)

[中文](recheck-0.22.md) · [Guarantee register](../guarantees.en.md) · [Session protocol specification](../protocol-session.md) · [Session trace contract](../formal-trace.en.md) · Issue: [#634](https://github.com/arcships/rutis/issues/634)

Status: first version. Date: 2026-11-15. Baseline: `origin/main` `a2a7a743`.

Generated by `node tools/formal-trace.mjs --write`; `--check` compares this page with `rules/trace-contract.md`, `rules/protocol-registry-1.md`, `rules/protocol-registry-2.md`, `rules/protocol-session.md`, `rules/protocol-events.md`, `rules/protocol-runtime.md`, `rules/protocol-remote.md` and the data in `tools/formal-trace-semantic.mjs`. 7 partitions have been re-extracted semantically: the 44 rows of the session trace contract (`TRC-*`), rewritten whole, and the 165, 218, 350, 262, 309 and 248 rows of the protocol lists `registry-1`, `registry-2`, `session`, `events`, `runtime`, `remote`, where the rule wording is still the issue snapshot's and the source, anchor, evidence and status are re-extracted. The other protocol partitions () were re-checked for source, anchor, evidence and status; their rule text has not been re-extracted, and their per-rule tables are in the Chinese page.

## 1. Method

The protocol rules pass four checks, and the trace rows pass a fifth. Every check that can be automated is in the generated pages; the judgement is in `tools/formal-trace-recheck.mjs` and `tools/formal-trace-semantic.mjs`.

1. **The source still exists**: the source document is in the current tree and 0.19 / 0.22 did not remove what it describes. Removed mechanisms (compat format, `events-v2`, the `sync-wait` capability, node `export` / `import`, `Registration`, the access point, `Revocations`, `PeerRows`, `peer:` rows, `runtimes.remote`, `Transport`, `ChannelInfo`) are `withdrawn`, and the gap names the rule that replaced them.
2. **A current anchor**: session rules map onto the `SESS-*` ids of `docs/protocol-session.md` §11; a registry row maps onto the guarantee-register row the issue split; an event rule maps onto the event protocol specification's own section or §15 decision, a `SESS-*` rule where the session specification carries it, or a register row; a runtime rule maps onto the guarantee-register row its 对应 column names, a `SESS-*` rule where the session specification states the synchronous wait, or a design section that is still the source; a remote rule maps onto the guarantee-register row its 对应 column names, a `SESS-*` rule where the session specification carries it, or a section of the remote-plugin, dial-out and app-integration designs or the connecting-nodes guide; a row with no anchor says why.
3. **The evidence resolves**: the paths, test names and line ranges of the evidence column are checked against the current tree; a rule with only a model, or without an implementation check, is `partial`.
4. **The status only moves down**: the issue snapshot is the ceiling. A rule is never raised to `verified` because a specification calls it current; current describes the rule, not its implementation evidence.
5. **The rule text is re-extracted** (trace rows only): the wording is the current source's, not the old design's, and the row names the `SESS-*` ids that carry it — or says why the session specification does not cover it.

## 5. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `registry-1`

The rule wording of these 165 rows is the issue snapshot's (Chinese, `rules/protocol-registry-1.md`); it was not rewritten. Each row was re-extracted on four points: the guarantee-register row that is its current source, that row as the anchor, the current evidence for the split claim, and the status with its reason.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

## 6. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `registry-2`

The rule wording of these 218 rows is the issue snapshot's (Chinese, `rules/protocol-registry-2.md`); it was not rewritten. Each row was re-extracted on four points: the guarantee-register row that is its current source, that row as the anchor, the current evidence for the split claim, and the status with its reason.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

## 7. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `session`

These 350 rows are the issue's session section (SES, VAL, TAG, M1, CHN); their rule wording is the issue snapshot's and was not rewritten. Each row re-extracts four things: the current source (a `SESS-*` rule of the session specification §11, a guarantee-register row, a design section that is still current, or `0.22 has no such rule`), the current anchor (a `SESS-*` id or a register row), the current evidence for the rule, and the status with its reason. The status is the re-check's and only moves down.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SES-1-1 | partial | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52, SESS-46 | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/session/testing.rs::session`（hold / fire / drop） | The session specification carries this in `SESS-52` (objects travel by reference) and `SESS-46` (reference wire format and identity); the issue marked it partial, and only function references have a cross-implementation test, object references (`Kind::Object`) have none. |
| SES-1-2 | partial | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-46, SESS-52 | `crates/rutis-bridge/tests/value_roundtrip.rs::rust_releases_balance`、`::node_releases_balance`、`::python_releases_balance`（返回值里的函数） | `SESS-46`/`SESS-52` allow references in arguments, returns and event payloads; the issue marked it partial: Future and object references in returns and events have no formal test. |
| SES-1-3 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (0.22 has no Rust runner: a Rust plugin runs in the host process itself, so there are no two runner kinds) | — | The runner design was never implemented (#121); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-1-4 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §十四 | — (The session specification does not cover it: M1 §十四 announces services that were provided without a declaration, so the “zero changes” exception is gone) | — | M1 §十四 and value design V0 mean a plugin need not be rewritten for the process boundary; the issue marked it replaced and 0.22 no longer states the limitation. |
| SES-1-5 | withdrawn | design-events-protocol-2026-10-18.md §十 | — (The session specification does not cover events: the event protocol specification §10 states the value and object rules) | — | The unified event protocol took over event values and objects, which SES §十一 itself says it superseded; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-1 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-38 | — | 0.22 allows a synchronous wait: an invocation frame carries `sync` and the waiting side blocks while it pumps frames (`SESS-38`), so “always asynchronous” no longer holds; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-2 | unverified | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52 | — | `SESS-52` sends objects with methods by reference rather than serialising them; the issue marked it uncovered, and the Node and Python object and dataclass behaviour has scattered ordinary tests only. |
| SES-2-3 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-6 | — | `get` is a live read and may carry `sync` (`SESS-6` lists the frame fields); the immutable-snapshot / asynchronous-getter restriction is gone; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-4 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-46 | — | There are no permission views: reference identity is the session plus the reference number with `home` for the original (`SESS-46`), so comparing across views has no subject; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-5 | verified | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52 | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/session/testing.rs::session`（apply：回调在原进程执行） | `SESS-52` sends functions and objects by reference, never their source; the issue marked it covered, and the `apply` case of all seven implementations is the test above. |
| SES-2-6 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-51 | — | The session layer clears the reference table when the session ends (`SESS-51`); unloading a row is a loader row rule; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-7 | partial | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52, SESS-6 | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/session/testing.rs::malformed`（未知 op 结束会话） | The frames are `call` / `get` / `await` only (`SESS-6`), and a proxy exposes no field writes, prototype chain or `instanceof` (`SESS-52`); the issue marked it partial because only an ordinary test checks that `instanceof` does not hold. |
| SES-2-8 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-6 | — | There are no interface descriptors or materialisation; `get` is a live read (`SESS-6`); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-1 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §五 | — (The session specification does not cover it: a managed plugin is a loader row plus a row inside the runtime (M1 §五), not a proxy fiber plus a runner fiber) | — | A managed plugin is now a loader row and a row inside the runtime (M1 §五); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-2 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/announced-services, loader/rows-gate-on-services | — | A service slot is replaced or withdrawn per M1 §5.3, and a row that depends on it is stopped by the loader gating rules (`loader/announced-services`, `loader/rows-gate-on-services`); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-3 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no activation or execution gate: loading is `rows.load` / `rows.unload`, with no stage a host start has to open) | — | `rows.load` / `rows.unload` replaced activation; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-4 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no activation: loading by generation and a late `rows.ended` replaced it (#625)) | — | A local refresh no longer has an “old activation”; generations and a late `rows.ended` are #625; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-5 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/rows-gate-on-services | — | A row whose dependency went away stops by the loader gating rule and reports `rows.ended`; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-1 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no runner group: one runtime per language, with more started when isolation is needed (multilanguage runtimes design §四)) | — | There is one runtime per language and no shared runner; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-2 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/reload-all-or-nothing | — | There is no runner group: `loader/reload-all-or-nothing` states the reload semantics, without dropping the module cache to pretend an unload; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-3 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no Rust runner or factory table, so a missing `factory` has no subject) | — | There is no Rust runner or factory table; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-4 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §5.1 | — (The session specification does not cover it: `rows.schema` reads `inject` from the loaded module without running the plugin (M1 §5.1)) | — | `rows.schema` and M1 §5.1 replaced the prepare / build stages; the issue marked it replaced. |

## 8. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `events`

These 262 rows are the issue's events section (EVP, EVD): 253 current and 9 replaced, with the rule wording left as the issue snapshot's. Each row re-extracts four things: the current source (a section or decision of the event protocol specification, a `SESS-*` rule of the session specification §11, a guarantee-register row, or `0.22 has no such rule`), the current anchor (`EVP` / `EVD` section or decision number, a `SESS-*` id, a register row), the current evidence (the conformance suite's event cases, the TLA+ invariants and mutation configurations under `specs/events`, a named Rust test), and the status with its reason. The status is the re-check's and only moves down. The event protocol specification is a 0.18 baseline: §2.1's `events-v2`, §2.2, the `events` field of §2.4 and §9's `export` / `import` were replaced by 0.19, and those rows are withdrawn.

## 9. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `runtime`

These 309 rows are the issue's runtime section (SYN, M2, MNT, REQ, RTP, INS, MSV): 264 current and 45 replaced, with the rule wording left as the issue snapshot's. Each row re-extracts four things: the current source (a `SESS-*` rule of the session specification §11, a guarantee-register row, a design section that is still current, or `0.22 has no such rule`), the current anchor (a `SESS-*` id or a register row), the current evidence, and the status with its reason. The status is the re-check's and only moves down. The runtime, mount, requirements and instance designs are still current sources; the multilanguage runtime design is a 0.9 baseline that 0.19 did not rewrite, and the mechanisms it lost (`emits`, `node:` call ids, the `sync-wait` capability, `TypeKey` keys, `dlopen`) are withdrawn.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SYN-2-1 | not counted | 0.22 has no such rule | — (No current document rewrites the runtime's process structure: the multilanguage runtime design is still the 0.9 baseline, and `Launcher::start` with `Process` provides the structure (`crates/rutis-bridge/src/runtime/process.rs`)) | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/runtime/mod.rs`（运行时是会话的对端，`Launcher::start` 起一个进程） | The 0.9 design was not rewritten against 0.22, so the process structure lives in the code only; the process count is an operating-system observation; the issue marked it inapplicable, so it is not counted. |

## 10. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `remote`

These 248 rows are the issue's remote section (REM, DIA, APP, NOD): 231 current and 17 replaced, with the rule wording left as the issue snapshot's. Each row re-extracts four things: the current source, the current anchor, the current evidence, and the status with its reason. The current source is one of four kinds: a guarantee-register row (where the `对应` column names a row the register still has, the entry cites that section), a section of the remote-plugin, dial-out and app-integration designs or the connecting-nodes guide (this partition's own documents), a `SESS-*` rule of the session specification, or `0.22 has no such rule` (the `Transport`, `Identity`, `Registration`, access point, `Revocations`, `PeerRows`, `peer:` rows, `runtimes.remote` and node export / import that 0.19 removed). Anchors are register rows and `SESS-*` rules only, so a row whose source is a design section carries none and says why. Connection behaviour after 0.19 is settled by `design-remote-session-2026-10-28.md`, and the remote plugin design's connection, identity, registration and node-list parts are withdrawn by that design's §8 and §12. The status is the re-check's and only moves down.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| REM-1-1 | unverified | guarantees.md §14 | loader/remote-runtime-rows | `crates/rutis-host/tests/remote_runtimes.rs::a_dial_row_runs_the_rows_of_a_runtime_that_listens` | The register row `loader/remote-runtime-rows` (§14) records that a row on the far side of a connection is managed like a local runtime row; the conformance suite the issue asked for was never built, the old `crates/rutis-loader/tests/remote_rows.rs` went with the connection plugin (`ec47944c`), and the current evidence is one ordinary dial-and-listen case; the issue marked it uncovered. |
| REM-1-2 | not counted | guarantees.md §14 | loader/remote-runtime-rows | `crates/rutis-host/tests/remote_runtimes.rs::a_dial_row_runs_the_rows_of_a_runtime_that_listens`（对面只装该语言运行时，本端跑 loader） | This is a deployment condition: a leaf end needs neither rutis nor a local dependency graph to be managed by the host's loader (`remote_runtimes.rs` uses listening Python, Bun and Go runtimes); the register row `loader/remote-runtime-rows` covers the neighbouring promise; the issue marked it inapplicable, so it is not counted. |
| REM-1-3 | partial | guarantees.md §14 | loader/peer-rows | `crates/rutis-host/tests/nodes.rs::two_hosts_as_nodes_share_services_events_and_run_rows` | Each node manages its own plugins and a far end can only have them installed through host: the register row `loader/peer-rows` (§14) is the current source, and `nodes.rs` covers two nodes sharing services, events and hosted rows; that a node's own plugins are untouched by the far end would need the hosted-row model (T6, deferred by #612) and has no formalisation; the issue marked it partial. |
| REM-1-4a | not guaranteed | design-remote-plugins-2026-10-03.md §1 | — (A scope statement: §1 of the remote plugin design lists what is explicitly not done (code distribution, a plugin marketplace, a deployment platform, an operating-system sandbox, independent forced termination), and neither the guarantee register nor the session specification states a rule for it) | — | The rule's own wording says these five things are not done, so it is an explicit non-guarantee and not counted as unverified; the issue marked it inapplicable. |
| REM-1-4b | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — ("rutis never restarts a runtime process" was replaced by `restart`: the default `no` does not restart, `on-failure` restarts after a wait (register row `host/runtime-restart`)) | — | Each runtime's `restart` now decides, and the default still does not restart; the current evidence is `crates/rutis-host/tests/runtimes.rs::a_runtime_restarted_on_failure_brings_its_rows_back`; the issue marked it replaced. |
| REM-1-4c | partial | guarantees.md §10 | bridge/no-call-on-a-dead-session, bridge/in-flight-call-fails-once | `crates/rutis-bridge/tests/fault_links.rs::calls_after_a_connection_dropped_fail_or_reach_the_new_session`、`::a_call_in_flight_when_the_connection_drops_fails_and_ran_once`；TLA：`specs/remote-lease/RemoteLease.tla::OldCommandsStayOld` | No session recovery: a reconnect is a new session that restores no old reference and replays no call whose result was unknown. The two register rows (§10) are the current source; the lease model builds row commands only, not references and in-flight calls, and "no replay" is planned as `SettlesOnce` (#626); the issue marked it partial. |
| REM-1-4d | partial | guarantees.md §14 | loader/remote-runtime-lease | `crates/rutis-host/tests/lease_e2e.rs::successive_controllers_each_get_a_clean_lease`；TLA：`specs/remote-lease/RemoteLease.tla::OneLease` | There is no grace period for a lost controller: a session's end cleans its lease at once instead of waiting for the far end (register row `loader/remote-runtime-lease`). The model treats the cleanup as instantaneous; on takeover Node and Bun kill the old child after the cleanup deadline (`node/rutis-runtime/src/serve.mjs`, `bun/rutis-bun/src/remote.ts`), and the grace itself has no dedicated case; the issue marked it partial. |
| REM-2-1 | partial | guarantees.md §15 | host/killed-host-leaves-no-runtimes | `crates/rutis-bridge/tests/host_gone.rs::node_exits_after_its_cleanups_when_its_host_goes_away`（Python、Go、Bun 各一例） | A leaf runtime the local parent started cleans every row and exits when the session ends, carried by the register rows `host/killed-host-leaves-no-runtimes` (§15) and `loader/row-unload-leaves-nothing` (§14, rows unload before the runtime); `host_gone.rs` has Node, Python, Go and Bun columns and lacks Swift and Kotlin (a Swift plugin builds only on macOS, and Kotlin needs a JDK 17 and `:rutis-conformance:fatJar`, #624); Go's `listen:` and local channels install no SIGTERM handler (only the dialling `dial.go` does) while Swift's `serveRemote` does (see `host/killed-host-leaves-no-runtimes a`); the issue marked it partial. |
| REM-2-2 | unverified | guarantees.md §10 | bridge/websocket-refusal-categories | `crates/rutis-bridge/tests/websocket_cross.rs::rust_dialing_python_is_refused_by_category`（只有 Rust 拨 Python、Node） | A remote runtime accepts only a controller its configuration admits and its transport authenticates. The register row `bridge/websocket-refusal-categories` (§10) records the refusal categories (missing or wrong token, untrusted CA, subprotocol mismatch), not the whole accept-and-verify behaviour; the lease model takes a successful upgrade as authenticated and has no authentication-failure state; the Go, Bun, Swift and Kotlin listeners have no counterpart; the issue marked it uncovered. |
| REM-2-3 | partial | guarantees.md §14 | loader/remote-runtime-lease | `crates/rutis-host/tests/lease_e2e.rs::successive_controllers_each_get_a_clean_lease`、`crates/rutis-host/tests/lease_e2e.rs::go_controllers_each_get_a_clean_lease`、`crates/rutis-host/tests/lease_e2e.rs::bun_controllers_each_get_a_clean_lease`；TLA：`specs/remote-lease/RemoteLease.tla::OneLease` | A network leaf runtime stays up and serves one controller after another, with one active controller per instance at a time (register row `loader/remote-runtime-lease`). The model's correspondence table lists Python, Go and Node only, and Swift's `Remote/Listen.swift` and Kotlin's `internal/remote/Listen.kt` are unchecked (#615); the lease suite `crates/rutis-host/tests/lease_e2e.rs` has Python, Go and Bun columns and lacks Swift and Kotlin (a Swift plugin builds only on macOS, and Kotlin needs a JDK 17 and `:rutis-conformance:fatJar`, #624); the issue marked it partial. |

## 11. What is still not re-extracted

- No current protocol rule is left out: all 1368 current rows and all 184 replaced rows of `rules/protocol-*.md` have an entry here. What the entries do not do is rewrite the wording — it stays the issue snapshot's, and the tables above do not translate it.
- The 219 guarantee-register rows: generated from `docs/guarantees.md`, so the register's wording is the source, not a re-extraction.
- The 10 `ECA-*` rows: hand-written proposals of the event-close admission design, with no current specification behind them yet.
